Appendices
M
If alive, what does the deep one do? Does it know any spells to
further complicate the situation? Appendix B:
M
What does Bloom discover on studying his specimen? Do the
investigators watch as he dissects the deep one, provoking a
Sanity roll, 1/1D6 loss?
M Play Aids
Does the study show how closely related humans and deep ones
are? Are the investigators ready for such revelations?
M
What does Bloom want next? Another specimen? Perhaps, a
shoggoth… This section collects play aids for the Keeper, including a selection
of deep one and hybrid game profiles, deep one orientated spells,
DEEP DREAMS
useful tomes, and a handful of artifacts. Use these to help with
scenario ideas or just as useful resources when running games.
Set Up
TABLE 1: WEATHER GENERATOR
Use this seed once the investigators have made one or more trips
to Innsmouth or otherwise made a nuisance of themselves in While most Keepers can simply choose what the weather is
town, enough to draw the interest of the EOD. doing on any given day, the following provides a rough source
of inspiration, determined by month, with a percentage chance
Backstory
for rainfall, snow, and so on.
During a previous visit/exploit, at least one of the investigators
left something behind, probably by accident, such as a piece of Month Rainfall Snowfall Other
clothing or an item like a fountain pen, comb, or handkerchief,
January 40% 80% Strong winds possible*
and so on. Or it could simply be a splatter of blood or shell
casings. Either way, this thing left behind becomes a focus that February 30% 60% Strong winds possible*
the Order uses to Send Dreams intending to confound, scare, March 90% 30%
and trouble the investigator(s) concerned.
April 80% 10%
Key Points May 50% —
The EOD draws investigator(s) into a series of dreams wherein June 40% —
they travel around Innsmouth, then to Devil Reef, and finally,
July 30% —
into the depths to Y’ha-nthlei.
August 30% —
Possibilities
M September 40% —
In the dreams, the investigator(s) gradually take on the
October 80% — Strong winds possible*
Innsmouth Look (page 42), develop the ability to survive
underwater, and get the chance to explore hidden places in November 60% 30% Strong winds possible*
town and beyond.
M December 40% 60% Strong winds possible*
Such dreams may provide clues or hints that can later be
explored, but they also expose the dreamers to nightmares made
flesh, such as the deep ones themselves, their strange city, and *Strong winds may include “nor’easters” —sometimes hurricane-force
to terrors like shoggoths. winds, usually accompanied by heavy rain or snow.
M
All this provokes Sanity rolls with associated losses, although
as these are dream experiences, reduce such losses down to just
10% of the rolled Sanity costs. So, during a dream, add up
Sanity losses incurred and total them together at the end of the
dream; then divide the total by 10, rounding down, for Sanity
lost upon waking.
M
If an investigator dies in a dream, they immediately wake
and suffer 1D6 Sanity loss. The next night, they continue to
experience the horrifying dreams.
M
While the investigator(s) dream, EOD agents attempt to
track them down, using their personal items, if appropriate;
this may take a few days to weeks, depending on where the
investigator(s) is sleeping. Such agents are tasked with capturing
the investigator(s), although murder is an option where capture
becomes too difficult.
M
The dreams continue while the agents track the investigator(s)
down. If the agents fail to do so within one month, they give
up the chase, and the dreams stop.
M
Perhaps the investigators seek a way to stop or prevent the
dreams. What magic can they learn to do so? What artifact
do they hear about that could help them? And at what cost?
246

Mapulos & Shoggoth-Twsha
Called “mapulos” by the deep ones, these
are arcane devices, created for one purpose:
to control shoggoths. The mapulos are two
slime-covered balls, usually crafted from gold,
enabling control of one specific shoggoth. Thus,
the mapulos are paired to the shoggoth using an
opposed POW roll, with the shoggoth accepting
simple mental commands from the wielder, who
is known as a “shoggoth-twshas” or “twshas.”
Typically, twshas are deep ones possessing
above average POW. Control is gained via
an opposed POW roll, although control
is automatic if the deep one possesses 50
points of POW greater than the shoggoth. If
necessary, and at the Keeper’s discretion, the
twsha must succeed with a POW roll once
per week to remain in control and uneaten by
the mapulos (following). Only deep ones can
become genuine shoggoth-twshas, and a deep
one controller loses command of their terrifying
charge only if they fumble the POW roll.
A shoggoth-twsha remains with their
shoggoth until the day the mapulos eat them.
The usual demeanor of a twsha is restful, as the
mapulos process the deep one’s fatigue poisons,
so that the twsha never needs to rest nor sleep
and, in fact, dares not. If the controller becomes
incapacitated, falls unconscious, or is severely
injured, the Keeper may deem them unable to
maintain control.
Should the twsha lose a POW roll to
maintain control or if the shoggoth dies, the
mapulos immediately attack their wielder by
burrowing into their body and causing 1D3
damage per round, unless they are excised.
If the shoggoth is alive when control fails, it
usually attacks the twsha in a fit of rage and
revenge. To make matters even worse, should a
Jewelry of the Deep
258

Appendix A
twsha leave their shoggoth uncontrolled for 10
or more minutes, the shoggoth’s consciousness
breaks free, and the mapulos attack the wielder.
Murder of the twsha enrages the shoggoth,
even as the thing is set free. Death of the
twsha allows the mapulos to activate and begin
devouring the corpse. Normally, two deep ones
protect a twsha from unforeseen attacks (e.g.,
investigators), as the twsha cannot attack
physically, although they may use spells. Since
the shoggoth-twsha can spare no more than
10 minutes from control of their charge, they
attack as lethally as possible—the shoggoth is
usually the best weapon they have available.
Once paired mapulos have begun to devour
their user, they become useless for control
purposes and are usually fed to a shoggoth
thereafter.
Human Use of Mapulos
Humans picking up a paired set of mapulos
must win an opposed POW roll versus the
shoggoth; such control lasts for 30 minutes,
after which another opposed POW roll must
be made.
The mapulos stick like glue to the skin,
causing 1D3 damage per round, and cannot
simply be pulled off. And, naturally, both
mapulos must be removed to save the controller
should they fail an opposed POW roll. Quick-
thinking investigators could, in theory, with
help, sever their arms, to prevent the mapulos
from burrowing up their arms and into their
bodies, or else perform sudden surgery to
extract the devices.
Such dire work requires a successful
Medicine or Hard First Aid roll, and a Luck
roll to ensure the patient survives.
In addition, while commanding a shoggoth,
a human controller must make a Sanity roll
for every half hour of contact (1D4/1D8 loss).
A person suffering severe Sanity loss while
controlling a shoggoth can still direct that
shoggoth, though their control may be wildly
wielded and unhelpful to allies.
Trident or Spear of the Deep
Such weapons are prized by deep ones and
usually take the form of a three-pronged trident,
but may be spears. Each weapon may possess
different benefits, but all are magical in nature
and can affect creatures normally immune to
mundane weapons. A standard trident deals
1D6+1 damage, with a base skill of 25%.

THE DEEP ONES IN TOMES
Note: for Cthulhu in Mythos Tomes, see Appendix B, page
333, in the Cults of Cthulhu supplement, whence this section
draws its material.
Here, we assume an investigator is quickly skimming the pages
of a tome, looking for specific information about the deep ones,
rather than reading big chunks of the text. The reader does not
260

Appendix A
get a sense of a tome’s outlook, full contents, spells, or other
characteristics—they only focus down on material associated
with deep ones.
The time taken to use a tome for this type of referencing is
determined by the Keeper, although we recommend that a tome’s
number of weeks for full study is converted into minutes plus one
hour; thus, if using Al Azif in this manner—68 weeks for a Full
reading—the time spent researching with this tome would be
one hour plus 68 minutes, equaling two hours and eight minutes.
We recommend that the Keeper provide one piece of
information per period of research. To find another useful tidbit
requires the same amount of time again. Such time is elastic, and
the Keeper may choose to increase or decrease it to suit the story
being told. Likewise, granting two or three pieces of information,
rather than one, may be necessary for the story being created.
The Keeper may choose to use the percentage chances offered
in the Cults of Cthulhu supplement, or instead, simply use a Luck
roll to determine if the tome has anything useful in it. If the
scenario requires that the investigators must obtain certain
information from a tome, dispense with the roll and simply
provide the necessary information to ensure the scenario moves
forward. In most cases, the information gained usually imposes
a Sanity cost, usually 1–1D4 Sanity points, although particularly
dire knowledge may increase the loss to 1D6 or 1D8 points.
Written Details
The information found in a tome should either provide
background information to the deep ones—their general beliefs,
goals, or nature—or be specific and pertinent to the current
scenario or campaign. If the latter, the Keeper should determine
the information beforehand to ensure it works with the emerging
story and does not reveal too many secrets too early in the game.
Some suggested information follows for more generic “secrets”—
the Keeper is advised to alter these as necessary to conform with
their depiction of Innsmouth, the deep ones, and so on.
What the Tome Says
M
Artifacts associated with or which work against the deep ones.
M
Concerning Father Dagon and Mother Hydra (their roles,
history, the powers they possess; see Who Is Dagon?, page 31).
M
Deep one ceremonies and rituals.
M
Deep one cults (histories, beliefs, or goals).
M
Deep one jewelry, especially items possessing unique powers.
M
Deep one origins, history, or connection to humanity.
M
Details about Y’ha-nthlei or another deep one city (history,
location, or other useful matter).
M
Enemies of Cthulhu (history, powers, or goals).
M
Information about shoggoths or other entities connected to
the deep ones.
M
Variations of deep ones (their differing forms, abilities, powers).
M
Prophecies concerning the deep ones.
M
Prophecies concerning the coming of Cthulhu or Cthulhu’s
arrival in pre-history.
M
Spells and other special workings designed to help or hinder
the deep ones.

WILLIAM J. BURNS J. EDGAR HOOVER
DIRECTOR DEPUTY
U. S. Department of Justice
BBureau of IInvestigation
WWashington, D. C.
MEMORANDUM FOR THE DIRECTOR
Additional evidence has been gathered suggesting bootlegging
activities are being carried out in the small town of Innsmouth on
the northern Massachusetts coast. The town is small, out of the
way, and receives little outside traffic. Has a small harbor of
sufficient size to allow for raw materials to be brought in and
product to be exported in quantities of concern without drawing much
attention.
On the fifth of May, Bureau agents arrested Milton Chancery in
Newburyport, Mass. with 38 cases of low quality gin. Once in cus-
tody, agents questioned subject regarding the source of the contra-
band. Initially claimed to have bought it off a cargo ship out of
Montreal whose name he could not recall, but when urged to reflect
more carefully stated it was from a Water street warehouse in the
town of Innsmouth. Claimed his contact there was a sailor named
Bicknell.
Chancery’s statement is of interest as it cross references
with a lead our office acquired from a C.I. operating out of Arkham,
Mass. some 12 miles southwest of Innsmouth. The informant, Fran-
cis “Little Frankie” Keisling, said that the O’Bannion crime gang in
Arkham has been bringing in increasing amounts of liquor from Inns-
mouth. The waterfront distillery has been operating at high capac-
ity and O’Bannion’s Lucky Clover Cartage Company has been moving
loads out of Innsmouth as far north as Portsmouth and as far south
as the Boston city limits. The distillery’s able to operate with
enough brass that they’re labeling their product.
Word on Innsmouth is it’s one of these tight-lipped small town
kind of places with no Volstead enforcement. Apparently there’s
some kind of gold refinery that might be laundering proceeds.
Getting an observer in place will take some patience. An agent with
a fishing or sailing background might be able to get a casual look
without tipping off the distillers. A few weeks of surveillance
should give us a better idea of the extent of the operation there
and what action the Bureau should undertake.
Respectfully submitted,
Agt. Thornton Hughes
